<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Let <i>T</i> be an invertible mixing probability-preserving transformation. For every integer <i>n</i> ≥ 2 and every fixed tuple of bounded measurable functions, we prove that the consecutive multiple ergodic averages of length <i>n</i> converge almost everywhere to the product of the integrals, as the averaging length tends to infinity through all positive integers. The probability space need not be standard, and no rate of mixing is required.

- **claim-main-pointwise-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Suppose that $T$ satisfies <a href="#eq:mixing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mixing">[eq:mixing]</a>. For every integer $n\ge2$ and every fixed $f_1,\ldots,f_n\in L^\infty(\mu)$, $$A_N(f_1,\ldots,f_n)(x) \longrightarrow\prod_{j=1}^n\int_X f_j\,d\mu \qquad\text{for $\mu$-almost every }x,$$ as $N$ tends to infinity through all positive integers. The probability space need not be standard, and no rate of mixing is required.
  - Explanation: For a mixing invertible transformation, every consecutive multiple average of bounded functions converges almost everywhere to the product of the integrals, with no standardness of the space and no mixing rate needed.
- **claim-higher-order-mixing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-multiple-mixing)): Let $T$ be an invertible mixing probability-preserving transformation. For any fixed $s\ge2$, $g_1,\ldots,g_s\in L^\infty(\mu)$, and integer times $t_1,\ldots,t_s$, $$\int_X\prod_{j=1}^s g_j(T^{t_j}x)\,d\mu(x) \longrightarrow\prod_{j=1}^s\int_Xg_j\,d\mu \quad\text{as}\quad \min_{i\ne j}\|t_i-t_j\|\longrightarrow\infty.$$ For $s=1$ the equality holds for every time.
  - Explanation: Companion input: ordinary mixing implies mixing of every finite order when the pairwise time gaps tend to infinity.
- **claim-smooth-triple-averages** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-smooth-input)): Let $R$ be any invertible probability-preserving transformation, and let $b_1,b_2,b_3$ be distinct nonzero integers with greatest common divisor one. For bounded $g_1,g_2,g_3$ and every averaging profile, $$V_k^\varphi(y)=\sum_{l\in\mathbb Z}2^{-k}\varphi(l/2^k) \prod_{j=1}^3g_j(R^{b_jl}y)$$ converges almost everywhere as $k\to\infty$. Moreover, for each $c\in[1,2]$ and $\epsilon>0$ there is such a profile with $$\lVert\varphi-c^{-1}\mathbf 1_{(0,c]}\rVert_{L^1(\mathbb R)}<\epsilon.$$
  - Explanation: Companion input: smooth dyadic triple averages at distinct slopes converge almost everywhere, and indicator-type profiles can be approximated in L1 by admissible profiles.
- **claim-relative-structure** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-structure)): The following statements hold for standard probability-preserving systems, without an ergodicity assumption.* 1. *Every system $Y$ has a maximal measure-distal factor $W_Y$, and $Y\to W_Y$ is relatively weakly mixing. Every distal factor pulls into the maximal distal factor of an extension. Automorphisms commuting with the transformation preserve $W_Y$.* 2. *Factors and joinings of distal systems are distal. A distal system is relatively distal over each of its factors. Distality is equivalent for a transformation and any of its nonzero integer powers.* 3. *Relative weak mixing passes to nonzero integer powers. The conditional product of relatively weakly mixing fibre laws over an invariant joining of their base laws is relatively weakly mixing over that joining.* 4. *A relatively weakly mixing extension and a relatively distal extension of the same base are relatively disjoint: every invariant joining identifying their bases is the conditional product over that base.* 5. *For a distal transformation $R$, every $d\ge1$, and bounded $g_1,\ldots,g_d$, the averages $$\frac1N\sum_{l=1}^N\prod_{i=1}^d g_i\circ R^{il}$$ converge almost everywhere.* *For a flow, the maximal distal factor of time one is preserved by the flow, and its action at every nonzero rational time is distal.
  - Explanation: Nonergodic structure facts: maximal distal factor, relative weak mixing, relative disjointness, and pointwise convergence on distal systems.
- **claim-relative-norm-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-relative-norm)): Suppose $Y\to B$ is relatively weakly mixing for $R$. For distinct nonzero integers $a_1,\ldots,a_d$ and bounded $G_1,\ldots,G_d$, $$\label{eq:relative-norm} \left\\|\frac1N\sum_{l=1}^N \left(\prod_{i=1}^dG_i\circ R^{a_il} -\prod_{i=1}^d\mathbb E[G_i\mid B]\circ R^{a_il}\right)\right\\|_2 \longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Over a relatively weakly mixing extension, multiple averages along distinct slopes may be replaced by the same averages of conditional expectations, up to an L2 error tending to zero.
- **claim-triple-pointwise-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-triple-convergence)): On any standard invertible probability-preserving system, averages of at most three bounded functions at distinct positive integer slopes converge almost everywhere along all positive integer lengths.
  - Explanation: Averages of at most three bounded functions at distinct positive slopes converge almost everywhere along all lengths on any standard system, with no mixing assumption.
- **claim-selected-positive-witness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-selected-witness)): There are a finite set $F\subseteq[1,2]$ containing $1$ and measurable functions $N_a:C\to[2^a,\infty)$, $a\in\mathbb N$, independent of the phase, with $$\label{eq:selector-grid} N_a(x,u)\in\{c2^k:c\in F,\ k\ge a\},\qquad N_a(x,u)=2^a\quad(x\notin E_*),$$ such that $B_{\lfloor N_a(x,u)\rfloor}(x)>\delta$ on $E_*$. Moreover, there is a continuous nonnegative function $\Psi$ on $(\mathbb R/\mathbb Z)^I$ for which $$\label{eq:selected-positive-limit} \liminf_{a\to\infty}\int_C\frac1{N_a(z)} \int_0^{N_a(z)} \Psi\bigl((\upsilon(S_C^{it}z))_{i\in I}\bigr) \prod_{i\in J_0}f_i\bigl(\xi(S_C^{it}z)\bigr) \,\,dt\,\,dm_C(z)>0.$$
  - Explanation: If convergence fails, measurable length selectors on the suspension together with a continuous phase test give a retained positive limiting integral.
- **claim-selected-joining-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-selected-law)): Every flow $Y$ over $C$ has a unique probability law $\lambda_Y$ on $Y^I$ satisfying, for all bounded measurable functions $G_i$ on $Y$, $$\label{eq:selected-law} \int_{Y^I}\prod_{i\in I}G_i(y_i)\,\,d\lambda_Y =\lim_{a\to\mathcal U}\int_Y\frac1{N_a(\pi_Yy)} \int_0^{N_a(\pi_Yy)}\prod_{i\in I}G_i(S_Y^{it}y) \,\,dt\,\,dm_Y(y).$$ Its marginals are $m_Y$, and it is invariant under $(y_i)_i\mapsto(S_Y^{it}y_i)_i$, $t\in\mathbb R$. If $\rho:Y'\to Y$ is an extension over $C$, then $(\rho^I)_*\lambda_{Y'}=\lambda_Y$.* *Countable inverse limits of extensions over $C$ exist in this category. The selected law at such a limit projects to the selected law at every stage. Pullbacks of the stage selected-law spaces have dense union in $L^2$ of the limit selected law.
  - Explanation: Each flow over the suspension carries a unique selected joining law built by an ultralimit; it has the flow as marginals, is invariant under speed-weighted times, and is functorial in extensions and inverse limits.
- **claim-parameter-small-marginals** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-selected-parameters)): For every flow $Y$ over $C$ and bounded measurable $H_i$ on $W_Y$, $$\label{eq:deterministic-parameters} \mathbb E_{\lambda_Y}\prod_{i\in I}H_i(P_i) =\lim_{L\to\infty}\int_Y\frac1L\int_0^L \prod_{i\in I}H_i\bigl(\omega_Y(S_Y^{it}y)\bigr) \,\,dt\,\,dm_Y(y).$$ For every nonempty $J\subseteq I$ with $\lvert J\rvert\le3$, $$\label{eq:small-conditional-law} \mathcal L_{\lambda_Y}\bigl((y_i)_{i\in J}\mid P\bigr) =\bigotimes_{i\in J}m_{Y,P_i}.$$
  - Explanation: The distal parameter law is computed by deterministic-length averages, and every selected marginal on a small set of slots is a product of fibre laws given the full parameter array.
- **claim-normalized-joining-flow** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-normalized-joining)): For $b\in I$, define $$\label{eq:normalized-joining} \mathsf M_b(Y)= \left(Y^I,\lambda_Y, S_{\mathsf M_b(Y)}^t(y_i)_i=(S_Y^{it/b}y_i)_i\right).$$ This is a standard probability-preserving flow over $C$ via $(y_i)_i\mapsto\pi_Y(y_b)$, and projection onto slot $b$ is an extension onto $Y$. The array $P$ belongs to the maximal measure-distal factor of $\mathsf M_b(Y)$ for time one.
  - Explanation: The selected law space becomes a flow by rescaling time at slot b, keeping slot b as a factor onto the original flow and keeping the parameter array in its maximal distal factor.
- **claim-retained-positive-correlation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-positive-witness)): With the functions and phase test of Lemma <a href="#lem:selected-witness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:selected-witness">7</a>, $$\label{eq:positive-witness} \int_{C^I}\Psi\bigl((\upsilon(z_i))_i\bigr) \prod_{i\in J_0}f_i\bigl(\xi(z_i)\bigr)\,\,d\lambda_C>0.$$ The phase array in this integral is measurable in the distal parameter array of $C^I$.
  - Explanation: The positive correlation recorded by the failure of convergence survives in the selected law on the suspension, and its phase factor is a function of the distal parameters.
- **claim-channel-conditioning** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-channel-conditioning)): For a channel $Y$, both identities in <a href="#eq:channel-laws" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:channel-laws">[eq:channel-laws]</a> hold with conditioning on its maximal measure-distal factor $W_Y$ in place of $V$. Every flow extension of $Y$ over $C$ is a channel with the pulled back output data, and the same strengthening holds there.
  - Explanation: The channel independence laws strengthen from conditioning on the phase coordinates to conditioning on the maximal distal factor, and they pass to extensions.
- **claim-channel-closure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-channel-closure)): Let $Y$ be a channel and $b\in I$. On $\mathsf M_b(Y)$ take $\pi_Y(y_b)$ as original factor and take every $D_\gamma(y_i)$, $i\in I$, as an output, in the old group of $\gamma$. These data make $\mathsf M_b(Y)$ a channel.
  - Explanation: The normalized joining of a channel, with all copied outputs and the slot-b original factor, is again a channel; the proof uses higher-order mixing and the tensor packing estimate.
- **claim-channel-copied-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-channel-copied-law)): For every channel $Y$, the copied spatial list $(d_Y(y_i))_{i\in I}$ under $\lambda_Y$ has its full product law conditional on $P=(\omega_Y(y_i))_{i\in I}$.
  - Explanation: The copied spatial outputs over all slots have a full product law given the distal parameter array.
- **claim-product-below-minimum-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-minimum-product)): On any channel, choose fewer than $k$ distinct slots and designate one allowed spatial list at each. Under its selected law, these lists, jointly, have their product spatial law conditional on the full distal parameter array.
  - Explanation: By minimality of the support size, any collection of fewer slots with allowed spatial lists has a product law given the parameter array.
- **claim-simultaneous-saturation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-saturation)): Every standard flow $Y$ over $C$ has a standard flow extension $Y_*\to Y$ over $C$ with the following property. For every further standard flow extension $\rho:Y'\to Y_*$ over $C$, every $f\in L^2(\lambda_{Y_*})$, and every $l\in\{0\}\cup I$, $$\mathbb E_{\lambda_{Y'}}[f\circ\rho^I\mid\mathcal D_l(Y')] =\mathbb E_{\lambda_{Y_*}}[f\mid\mathcal D_l(Y_*)]\circ\rho^I. \label{eq:saturation}$$
  - Explanation: Every standard flow over the suspension has an extension whose conditional expectations on the parameter data and single-slot data cannot change under any further extension.
- **claim-regrouped-channel** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-regrouped-channel)): The preceding output lists and groups make $Z$ a channel over its original factor $\pi_Y(y_b)$.
  - Explanation: Regrouping the other slots as outputs through a surjection onto the three groups gives a channel over the slot-b original factor.
- **claim-row-column-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-row-column)): Under $\lambda_Z$, $$\mathbb E\big[q((y_i)_i)q(\mathbf z_b)\mid E\big] =\int_Y g(p,y)u(E_b,y)\,\,dm_{Y,w}(y). \label{eq:row-column}$$ Moreover, in a single $Z$-state with law $m_Z$, the conditional law of its slot-$b$ state given $e$ is $m_{Y,\kappa(e)}$.
  - Explanation: Saturation relates the row and column correlations in the square: their conditional product pairs through integration over the corner fibre law.
- **claim-parameter-independence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-parameter-independence)): Under $\lambda_Z$, the random variables $p$ and $E_b$ are conditionally independent given $w$.
  - Explanation: Column parameters and the row parameter are conditionally independent given the common corner parameter.
- **claim-tensor-packing** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-packing)): There is a universal constant $C$ with the following property. Let $0<\varepsilon\le1$, and let $d\ge1$ and $L\ge0$ be integers. Let $x_1,\ldots,x_m$ and $y_1,\ldots,y_m$ lie in the unit balls of real Hilbert spaces $H_1,H_2$. For each family suppose that a graph on $\{1,\ldots,m\}$ of maximum degree at most $L$ contains every off-diagonal pair whose absolute inner product exceeds $\eta$, where $$0\le\eta\le\varepsilon^2/10^4.$$ The two exceptional graphs may differ. For every orthogonal projection $P$ on $H_1\otimes H_2$ of rank at most $d$, $$\label{eq:packing-quantitative} \#\{j:\lVert P(x_j\otimes y_j)\rVert\ge\varepsilon\} \le C(1+L)\varepsilon^{-2} \exp\!\left(C\varepsilon^{-2}\sqrt d\log(2+d)\right).$$ For $d=d_m$ as defined in <a href="#eq:channel-dimension" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:channel-dimension">[eq:channel-dimension]</a>, this is $o_{\varepsilon,L}(m)$, uniformly over the Hilbert spaces, vectors, projections, and allowed values of $\eta$.
  - Explanation: A low-rank projection captures large norm for only a sublinear number of almost orthogonal tensor products, with an explicit bound depending on the graph degree and the rank.
- **claim-gaussian-matrix-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-packing-gaussian)): Let $B_1,\ldots,B_r$ be matrices between two finite-dimensional real Hilbert spaces, and put $$H_j=\begin{pmatrix}0&B_j\\B_j^*&0\end{pmatrix}, \qquad S=\sum_{j=1}^rH_j^2.$$ If $\lVert S\rVert_{\rm op}\le\sqrt d$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}S\le2d$ for some $d\ge1$, then for independent standard normal variables $g_j$, $$\label{eq:packing-gaussian-bound} \mathbb E\left\\|\sum_{j=1}^rg_jB_j\right\\|_{\rm op} \le C d^{1/4}\sqrt{\log(2+d)}.$$ The constant is independent of the dimensions of the two spaces.
  - Explanation: The expected operator norm of a Gaussian combination of matrices is bounded in terms of the operator norm and trace of the squared block sum, independently of ambient dimensions.

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-structure",
        "exact_quote": "The following statements hold for standard probability-preserving systems, without an ergodicity assumption.*\n\n1.  *Every system $Y$ has a maximal measure-distal factor $W_Y$, and $Y\\to W_Y$ is relatively weakly mixing. Every distal factor pulls into the maximal distal factor of an extension. Automorphisms commuting with the transformation preserve $W_Y$.*\n\n2.  *Factors and joinings of distal systems are distal. A distal system is relatively distal over each of its factors. Distality is equivalent for a transformation and any of its nonzero integer powers.*\n\n3.  *Relative weak mixing passes to nonzero integer powers. The conditional product of relatively weakly mixing fibre laws over an invariant joining of their base laws is relatively weakly mixing over that joining.*\n\n4.  *A relatively weakly mixing extension and a relatively distal extension of the same base are relatively disjoint: every invariant joining identifying their bases is the conditional product over that base.*\n\n5.  *For a distal transformation $R$, every $d\\ge1$, and bounded $g_1,\\ldots,g_d$, the averages $$\\frac1N\\sum_{l=1}^N\\prod_{i=1}^d g_i\\circ R^{il}$$ converge almost everywhere.*\n\n*For a flow, the maximal distal factor of time one is preserved by the flow, and its action at every nonzero rational time is distal.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-selected-law",
        "exact_quote": "Every flow $Y$ over $C$ has a unique probability law $\\lambda_Y$ on $Y^I$ satisfying, for all bounded measurable functions $G_i$ on $Y$, $$\\label{eq:selected-law}\n \\int_{Y^I}\\prod_{i\\in I}G_i(y_i)\\,\\,d\\lambda_Y\n =\\lim_{a\\to\\mathcal U}\\int_Y\\frac1{N_a(\\pi_Yy)}\n   \\int_0^{N_a(\\pi_Yy)}\\prod_{i\\in I}G_i(S_Y^{it}y)\n   \\,\\,dt\\,\\,dm_Y(y).$$ Its marginals are $m_Y$, and it is invariant under $(y_i)_i\\mapsto(S_Y^{it}y_i)_i$, $t\\in\\mathbb R$. If $\\rho:Y'\\to Y$ is an extension over $C$, then $(\\rho^I)_*\\lambda_{Y'}=\\lambda_Y$.*\n\n*Countable inverse limits of extensions over $C$ exist in this category. The selected law at such a limit projects to the selected law at every stage. Pullbacks of the stage selected-law spaces have dense union in $L^2$ of the limit selected law.",
        "source_id": "PM-154.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-selected-law",
          "exact_quote": "Every flow $Y$ over $C$ has a unique probability law $\\lambda_Y$ on $Y^I$ satisfying, for all bounded measurable functions $G_i$ on $Y$, $$\\label{eq:selected-law}\n \\int_{Y^I}\\prod_{i\\in I}G_i(y_i)\\,\\,d\\lambda_Y\n =\\lim_{a\\to\\mathcal U}\\int_Y\\frac1{N_a(\\pi_Yy)}\n   \\int_0^{N_a(\\pi_Yy)}\\prod_{i\\in I}G_i(S_Y^{it}y)\n   \\,\\,dt\\,\\,dm_Y(y).$$ Its marginals are $m_Y$, and it is invariant under $(y_i)_i\\mapsto(S_Y^{it}y_i)_i$, $t\\in\\mathbb R$. If $\\rho:Y'\\to Y$ is an extension over $C$, then $(\\rho^I)_*\\lambda_{Y'}=\\lambda_Y$.*\n\n*Countable inverse limits of extensions over $C$ exist in this category. The selected law at such a limit projects to the selected law at every stage. Pullbacks of the stage selected-law spaces have dense union in $L^2$ of the limit selected law.",
          "source_id": "PM-154.1"
        }

        "exact_quote": "For every flow $Y$ over $C$ and bounded measurable $H_i$ on $W_Y$, $$\\label{eq:deterministic-parameters}\n \\mathbb E_{\\lambda_Y}\\prod_{i\\in I}H_i(P_i)\n =\\lim_{L\\to\\infty}\\int_Y\\frac1L\\int_0^L\n   \\prod_{i\\in I}H_i\\bigl(\\omega_Y(S_Y^{it}y)\\bigr)\n   \\,\\,dt\\,\\,dm_Y(y).$$ For every nonempty $J\\subseteq I$ with $\\lvert J\\rvert\\le3$, $$\\label{eq:small-conditional-law}\n \\mathcal L_{\\lambda_Y}\\bigl((y_i)_{i\\in J}\\mid P\\bigr)\n   =\\bigotimes_{i\\in J}m_{Y,P_i}.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-packing",
        "exact_quote": "There is a universal constant $C$ with the following property. Let $0<\\varepsilon\\le1$, and let $d\\ge1$ and $L\\ge0$ be integers. Let $x_1,\\ldots,x_m$ and $y_1,\\ldots,y_m$ lie in the unit balls of real Hilbert spaces $H_1,H_2$. For each family suppose that a graph on $\\{1,\\ldots,m\\}$ of maximum degree at most $L$ contains every off-diagonal pair whose absolute inner product exceeds $\\eta$, where $$0\\le\\eta\\le\\varepsilon^2/10^4.$$ The two exceptional graphs may differ. For every orthogonal projection $P$ on $H_1\\otimes H_2$ of rank at most $d$, $$\\label{eq:packing-quantitative}\n \\#\\{j:\\lVert P(x_j\\otimes y_j)\\rVert\\ge\\varepsilon\\}\n \\le C(1+L)\\varepsilon^{-2}\n       \\exp\\!\\left(C\\varepsilon^{-2}\\sqrt d\\log(2+d)\\right).$$ For $d=d_m$ as defined in <a href=\"#eq:channel-dimension\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:channel-dimension\">[eq:channel-dimension]</a>, this is $o_{\\varepsilon,L}(m)$, uniformly over the Hilbert spaces, vectors, projections, and allowed values of $\\eta$.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $B_1,\\ldots,B_r$ be matrices between two finite-dimensional real Hilbert spaces, and put $$H_j=\\begin{pmatrix}0&B_j\\\\B_j^*&0\\end{pmatrix},\n \\qquad S=\\sum_{j=1}^rH_j^2.$$ If $\\lVert S\\rVert_{\\rm op}\\le\\sqrt d$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}S\\le2d$ for some $d\\ge1$, then for independent standard normal variables $g_j$, $$\\label{eq:packing-gaussian-bound}\n \\mathbb E\\left\\|\\sum_{j=1}^rg_jB_j\\right\\|_{\\rm op}\n       \\le C d^{1/4}\\sqrt{\\log(2+d)}.$$ The constant is independent of the dimensions of the two spaces.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **mixing transformation** ([source](#eq:mixing)): We say that $T$ is *mixing* if $$\label{eq:mixing} \mu(A\cap T^{-r}B)\longrightarrow\mu(A)\mu(B) \qquad (\|r\|\to\infty)$$ for every $A,B\in\mathcal F$.
- **relatively compact extension** ([source](#pm-h-7)): An extension is *relatively compact* if the invariant finitely generated $L^\infty(B)$-modules span a dense subspace of its $L^2$ space.
- **relatively distal extension** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A *relatively distal* extension is a tower of relatively compact extensions and inverse limits.
- **distal system** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A system is *distal* if it is distal over the trivial factor.
- **joining** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A joining is an invariant probability law on a product with the prescribed marginals.
- **flow over the suspension** ([source](#pm-h-11)): A *flow over $C$* is a standard probability space $(Y,m_Y)$ with a jointly Borel probability-preserving action $S_Y^t$, $t\in\mathbb R$, and a probability-preserving factor map $\pi_Y:Y\to C$ satisfying $\pi_Y S_Y^t=S_C^t\pi_Y$ modulo null sets for each fixed $t$.
- **channel** ([source](#stmt-def-channel)): A *channel* is a flow $Y$ over the suspension $C$, with factor map $\pi_Y:Y\to C$, together with a finite list of probability-preserving maps $D_\gamma:Y\to C$, called *outputs*, and positive rational numbers $q_\gamma$, such that $$D_\gamma(S_Y^t y)=S_C^{q_\gamma t}D_\gamma(y)$$ for each fixed $t$, modulo null sets.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-averaging-length | number of slots n in the consecutive average | ≥ | $2$ | every integer n at least two |
| q-mixing-order | order s of higher-order mixing | ≥ | $2$ | fixed s at least two, times with growing pairwise gaps |
| q-small-marginal-size | size of slot subsets with product conditional law | ≤ | $3$ | nonempty subsets J of the slot set |
| q-minimum-support | minimum support size k of a nonzero correlation | ≤ | $4\leq k\leq n$ | k between four and n |
| q-length-change | change of an average when the length changes from N to M | ≤ | $\frac{2\|M-N\|}{\max(M,N)}$ | any positive lengths M and N |
| q-failure-threshold | lower bound of the centered average along a failing sequence of lengths | > | $2\delta$ | arbitrarily large integers L |
| q-projection-dimension | rank bound d_m of the leading-eigenvector projection | = | $d_m=\left\lceil(1+\log m)^{3/2}\right\rceil$ | m the number of complete intervals |
| q-orthogonality-threshold | almost-orthogonality threshold eta | ≤ | $\varepsilon^2/10^4$ | eta nonnegative, epsilon in the stated range |
| q-gaussian-trace-bound | trace of the squared block sum S | ≤ | $2d$ | some d at least one |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-companion-higher-mixing** [claim](#stmt-thm-multiple-mixing) / [proof](#stmt-thm-multiple-mixing): Imported companion theorem, adapted from sets to bounded functions by approximation and ordering of times. -> mixing of every finite order
- **step-companion-smooth-triple** [claim](#stmt-prop-smooth-input) / [proof](#stmt-prop-smooth-input): Imported companion result on smooth dyadic triple averages and profile approximation. -> almost everywhere convergence of smooth triple averages
- **step-structure-facts** [claim](#stmt-prop-structure) / [proof](#stmt-prop-structure): Structure theory facts quoted from the fourfold companion and the cited literature. -> relative structure statements
- **step-relative-norm** [claim](#stmt-lem-relative-norm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-relative-norm): Telescoping, induction on the number of functions and the Hilbert-space van der Corput inequality, closed by relative weak mixing. -> L2 replacement by conditional expectations
- **step-triple-convergence** [claim](#stmt-lem-triple-convergence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-triple-convergence): Smooth dyadic averages along rational scales, Riemann sums, and a length-change estimate over a fine grid. -> triple pointwise convergence along all lengths
- **step-selected-witness** [claim](#stmt-lem-selected-witness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-selected-witness): Grid selection of lengths from the assumed failure and a phase test on the roof-one suspension. -> selectors and positive limiting integral
- **step-selected-law** [claim](#stmt-prop-selected-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-selected-law): Ultralimit of orbit measures, marginal convergence by Birkhoff, uniqueness from product tests, inverse limits by cylinders. -> selected joining law on every flow
- **step-small-marginals** [claim](#stmt-prop-selected-parameters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-selected-parameters): Pass from discrete to continuous time, use triple convergence and relative norm convergence, then relative disjointness for the full parameter array. -> parameter law and small-marginal product laws
- **step-normalized-joining** [claim](#stmt-lem-normalized-joining) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-normalized-joining): Invariance of the selected law after rescaling time, with distality of the parameter array from rational-time distal systems. -> normalized joining flow
- **step-positive-correlation** [claim](#stmt-lem-positive-witness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-positive-witness): Approximate the phase test by sums of product tests and apply the selected-law formula to the positive lower limit. -> retained positive correlation
- **step-channel-conditioning** [claim](#stmt-lem-channel-conditioning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-channel-conditioning): Relative weak mixing of the spatial factor over the phases plus relative disjointness from the distal factor. -> channel laws conditioned on the distal factor
- **step-tensor-packing** [claim](#stmt-thm-packing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-packing-gaussian-2): Discard directions of large variance by covering, then compare Gaussian processes and apply the Gaussian matrix estimate. -> quantitative packing bound
- **step-gaussian-estimate** [claim](#stmt-lem-packing-gaussian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-packing-gaussian): Gaussian integration by parts for high moments of the trace and a choice of moment order comparable to a logarithm. -> dimension-free operator norm bound
- **step-channel-closure** [claim](#stmt-prop-channel-closure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-channel-closure): Reduce to centered tests, use mixing of all orders for proper groups, and handle three groups by almost orthogonal vectors, rank projections and the packing bound summed over lengths. -> channel closure
- **step-channel-copied-law** [claim](#stmt-cor-channel-copied-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-channel-copied-law): Apply closure and conditioning to the normalized joining and condition down to the parameter array. -> copied outputs have product law
- **step-minimum-product** [claim](#stmt-lem-minimum-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-minimum-product): Expand tests into means and centered parts; every nonconstant term is a correlation with fewer slots than the minimum and vanishes. -> product laws below the minimum support
- **step-saturation** [claim](#stmt-lem-saturation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-saturation): Countable bounded-energy tower of extensions, inverse limit, and equality of nested conditional projections from equal norms. -> saturated extension
- **step-regrouped-channel** [claim](#stmt-lem-regrouped-channel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-regrouped-channel): Surjection onto the three groups, minimality of support and maximality of output roles to verify both channel laws. -> regrouped channel
- **step-row-column** [claim](#stmt-lem-row-column) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-row-column): Saturation with the parameter datum and with the single-slot datum, then the singleton fibre law. -> row-column identity
- **step-parameter-independence** [claim](#stmt-lem-parameter-independence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-parameter-independence): Parameter-law identity on the new flow with a measure-preserving change of variable at each time. -> conditional independence of the two parameter descriptions
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-parameter-independence-2): Contradiction: a nonzero pairing in a Hilbert space with independent copies gives a correlation with one more output role, against maximality; small n is excluded by the minimum support; standard models pull back to the original space. -> almost everywhere convergence to the product of means

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-main-ae-convergence**: For a mixing invertible probability-preserving transformation, for every length n at least two and every fixed tuple of bounded functions, the consecutive multiple averages converge almost everywhere to the product of the integrals, along all positive integer lengths, with no mixing rate and no standardness of the space assumed.
- **inv-triple-pointwise**: On a standard invertible system, averages of at most three bounded functions at distinct positive integer slopes converge almost everywhere along all positive integer lengths, without a mixing assumption.
- **inv-small-marginals-product**: Under the selected joining law, every marginal on at most three slots has the product conditional law given the full array of distal parameters.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-no-mixing**: Apply the arbitrary-length pointwise convergence to a system that is only ergodic or weakly mixing. -> The result is proved only under mixing; without it the product-of-integrals limit is not guaranteed by this theorem.
- **anti-quantitative-rate**: Read the theorem as giving an error rate or uniformity in the starting point. -> Only qualitative almost-everywhere convergence is asserted; no rate of mixing or of convergence is supplied.
- **anti-other-time-patterns**: Apply the theorem to averages along times other than consecutive multiples of the length. -> The proof concerns the specific times k, 2k, up to nk; other time patterns are not covered.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is an existence statement about almost-everywhere limits of averages on abstract measure spaces, proved by contradiction through ultralimits, selectors and saturated extensions. It is not a finite procedure; the packing theorem only asserts an unspecified universal constant.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-mixing-invertibility** (hypothesis): The main theorem is stated for an invertible, bimeasurable, measure-preserving transformation on a probability space that is mixing. A system that is only ergodic or weakly mixing, a non-invertible map, or a non-probability measure is outside it. Blocked use: ['Applying arbitrary-length pointwise convergence to a merely ergodic or weakly mixing system', 'Applying the result to a non-invertible endomorphism or an infinite-measure system']
- **limit-qualitative-only** (formal_scope): The conclusion is qualitative almost-everywhere convergence. The statement itself says no rate of mixing is required, so it supplies no convergence rate and no uniformity over starting points. Blocked use: ['Deriving an error bound or stopping time for a finite number of terms', 'Claiming convergence at every starting point']
- **limit-three-slot-regime** (edge_case): Without mixing, the pointwise input proved here covers only averages of at most three functions at distinct positive integer slopes on standard systems. Longer averages on arbitrary systems are not covered by this input. Blocked use: ['Using the triple convergence lemma for four or more slots on a non-mixing system', 'Using it on a non-standard space without first passing to a standard model']
- **limit-ultrafilter-selected-law** (nonconstructive): The selected joining laws are defined through a free ultrafilter limit. They are existence objects, not computable ones. Blocked use: ['Implementing or numerically computing the selected law', 'Treating the selected law as canonical independent of the ultrafilter choice']
- **limit-saturation-existential** (nonconstructive): The saturated extension is obtained from near-supremum choices over all extensions and a countable inverse limit. It is shown to exist but is not exhibited. Blocked use: ['Building the saturated extension as a concrete data structure', 'Using it to give an explicit witness']
- **limit-packing-constant** (approximation): The tensor packing bound holds with an unspecified universal constant, and its sublinearity is only asymptotic in the number of vectors. It gives no explicit numerical count. Blocked use: ['Using the bound to size a concrete computation', 'Quoting a numerical value for the constant']
- **limit-proof-by-contradiction** (nonconstructive): The main theorem is proved by contradiction against a maximal choice of the number of output roles. The argument gives no explicit procedure for locating a failure of convergence. Blocked use: ['Extracting an algorithm or a rate from the proof', 'Treating the proof as constructive']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Let <i>T</i> be an invertible mixing probability-preserving transformation. For every integer <i>n</i> ≥ 2 and every fixed tuple of bounded measurable functions, we prove that the consecutive multiple ergodic averages of length <i>n</i> converge almost everywhere to the product of the integrals, as the averaging length tends to infinity through all positive integers. The probability space need not be standard, and no rate of mixing is required.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

<a id="eq:mixing"></a>
Let $(X,\mathcal F,\mu)$ be a probability space and let $T:X\to X$ be invertible, bimeasurable, and measure preserving. We say that $T$ is *mixing* if $$\label{eq:mixing}
 \mu(A\cap T^{-r}B)\longrightarrow\mu(A)\mu(B)
 \qquad (|r|\to\infty)$$ for every $A,B\in\mathcal F$. For a fixed tuple of bounded measurable functions, the consecutive multiple ergodic averages are $$A_N(f_1,\ldots,f_n)(x)
 =\frac1N\sum_{k=1}^N\prod_{j=1}^n f_j(T^{jk}x).$$ The problem is to determine their almost-everywhere behavior, not merely their limit in norm. We prove the following result.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Suppose that $T$ satisfies <a href="#eq:mixing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mixing">[eq:mixing]</a>. For every integer $n\ge2$ and every fixed $f_1,\ldots,f_n\in L^\infty(\mu)$, $$A_N(f_1,\ldots,f_n)(x)
 \longrightarrow\prod_{j=1}^n\int_X f_j\,d\mu
 \qquad\text{for $\mu$-almost every }x,$$ as $N$ tends to infinity through all positive integers. The probability space need not be standard, and no rate of mixing is required.*

</div>

The exceptional null set may depend on the system and the fixed tuple. The theorem concerns the times $k,2k,\ldots,nk$ and does not require a common exceptional set for all bounded functions.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context and prior work

Arithmetic-progression averages became central to ergodic Ramsey theory through Furstenberg’s proof of Szemerédi’s theorem (Furstenberg 1977) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Furstenberg1977](#ref-Furstenberg1977)]. The distinction between norm and pointwise convergence is substantial. Host and Kra proved $L^2$ convergence of these averages for every finite length on arbitrary probability-preserving systems (Host and Kra 2005, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[HostKra2005](#ref-HostKra2005)]. Ziegler obtained another proof through universal characteristic factors (Ziegler 2007, Corollary 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Ziegler2007](#ref-Ziegler2007)]. For pointwise convergence, Bourgain’s double recurrence theorem treats two factors (Bourgain 1990) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Bourgain1990](#ref-Bourgain1990)]. Higher-length results have been obtained under additional structural assumptions: K-systems (Derrien and Lesigne 1996, Theorem 4.2) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[DerrienLesigne1996](#ref-DerrienLesigne1996)], weakly mixing systems with singular spectrum on their Pinsker factor (Assani 1998) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Assani1998](#ref-Assani1998)], and weakly mixing systems whose pairwise independent self-joinings are independent (Gutman et al. 2018, Theorem 3.4) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[GutmanHuangShaoYe2018](#ref-GutmanHuangShaoYe2018)]. Huang, Shao, and Ye proved pointwise convergence for every finite length on ergodic measure-distal systems (Huang, Shao, and Ye 2019, Theorem C) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[HuangShaoYe2019](#ref-HuangShaoYe2019)].

The present proof extends the fourfold mixing theorem of (OpenAI 2026a, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[FourfoldCompanion](#ref-FourfoldCompanion)]. It uses two substantial companion results. The first proves that ordinary mixing implies mixing of every finite order, the assertion of Rokhlin’s multiple-mixing problem (OpenAI 2026b, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[MixingCompanion](#ref-MixingCompanion)]. The second gives an oscillation estimate for triple averages at distinct integer slopes on arbitrary invertible probability-preserving systems (OpenAI 2026c, Proposition 3.5) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[SlopesCompanion](#ref-SlopesCompanion)]; together with its profile approximation, this yields pointwise triple convergence at distinct positive integer slopes without a mixing assumption. Section <a href="#sec:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:inputs">2</a> states their exact forms and derives the required triple pointwise theorem along all lengths. These companion proofs are not reproduced here. The selected-law construction, arbitrary-length extension argument, and tensor estimate used below are proved in full.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The arbitrary-length step

We briefly describe what must change when the number of factors increases. A failure of pointwise convergence allows an averaging length to be chosen measurably at each starting point so that a centered average stays positive. After passing to a suspension flow, these choices define a probability law on $n$ output coordinates. The law has the correct individual marginals and is invariant under different speeds in the different coordinates. The same construction works on extensions of the flow.

The method of (OpenAI 2026a, secs. 3–6) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[FourfoldCompanion](#ref-FourfoldCompanion)] organizes additional outputs into three groups. Their spatial coordinates are independent, and the original spatial coordinate is independent of any two groups; independence from all three groups is not imposed. We retain this three-group structure even when there are $n$ slots. Higher-order mixing and a tensor-packing estimate show that these independence properties survive the selected joining construction.

Two changes make this approach work for arbitrary $n$. First, we minimize the number of slots supporting a nonzero centered correlation. This gives independence for every smaller collection of the specific spatial lists being tested. A slot may read both the original spatial coordinate and the outputs in any proper subset of the three groups. That flexibility permits the outputs to be regrouped without imposing independence on arbitrary collections of whole states.

Second, an extension is chosen so that conditioning on all distal parameters together with a single state cannot improve under any further extension. This is a conditional-expectation energy construction, related to the sated-extension method (Austin 2015) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Austin2015](#ref-Austin2015)] and adapted from (OpenAI 2026a, sec. 6) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[FourfoldCompanion](#ref-FourfoldCompanion)]. Applying the joining construction again gives a square array. The single-state conditioning identity controls its distinguished row and column. Their parameter laws and a Hilbert-space argument then produce a new nonzero correlation with the same number of slots but one more output-only slot. Maximizing that number gives the contradiction. Only the pointwise theorem for at most three slots on arbitrary systems is used.

Section <a href="#sec:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:inputs">2</a> records the dynamical inputs. Section <a href="#sec:selected" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:selected">3</a> constructs the selected laws and their conditional marginals. Section <a href="#sec:channels" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:channels">4</a> proves preservation of the three-group structure. Sections <a href="#sec:correlations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:correlations">5</a> and <a href="#sec:square" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:square">6</a> carry out the minimal-correlation and square arguments. Appendix <a href="#sec:packing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:packing">7</a> proves the tensor estimate.

<a id="sec:inputs"></a>
### Dynamical inputs and standard models

Fix an integer $n\ge2$ and put $I=\{1,\ldots,n\}$. We first state the dynamical results used below. The distinction between norm and pointwise convergence matters: norm convergence is available for any finite number of slots, whereas the pointwise input on arbitrary systems will be used only for at most three slots.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Standard models and the two companion inputs

For the fixed tuple in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, record all integer translates of bounded measurable representatives of the functions. This gives a measurable map into a countable product of compact scalar discs, intertwining $T$ with the invertible shift. The pushforward measure defines a standard Borel probability system, and mixing passes to this factor. The original functions are its coordinate functions. Changing representatives affects at most a countable union of translated null sets, so almost-everywhere convergence on the factor pulls back. We may therefore work on standard probability spaces.

Throughout the proof, factors and conditional laws are understood modulo null sets. We use Borel versions of measurable functions. Sub-$\sigma$-algebras on a standard probability space have standard models modulo null sets. A flow is a jointly Borel action $(S_Y^t)_{t\in\mathbb R}$ on a standard Borel probability space $(Y,m_Y)$, preserving $m_Y$. Factor maps commute with every fixed time modulo null sets. Joint measurability permits Fubini in all time integrals. For a preserved factor, induced transformations at fixed times can be modeled as invertible maps; we take time integrals on the original flow.

We use the following statement from (OpenAI 2026b, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[MixingCompanion](#ref-MixingCompanion)].

<a id="stmt-thm-multiple-mixing"></a>
<div id="thm:multiple-mixing" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Higher-order mixing, companion input). *Let $T$ be an invertible mixing probability-preserving transformation. For any fixed $s\ge2$, $g_1,\ldots,g_s\in L^\infty(\mu)$, and integer times $t_1,\ldots,t_s$, $$\int_X\prod_{j=1}^s g_j(T^{t_j}x)\,d\mu(x)
 \longrightarrow\prod_{j=1}^s\int_Xg_j\,d\mu
 \quad\text{as}\quad
 \min_{i\ne j}|t_i-t_j|\longrightarrow\infty.$$ For $s=1$ the equality holds for every time.*

</div>

The cited theorem is stated for sets and ordered times. Simple-function approximation, invariance, and the finitely many orderings of the times give this formulation. No quantitative mixing rate is asserted or used.

Our second companion input is the smooth-average convergence consequence of (OpenAI 2026c, Proposition 3.5 and Lemma 3.6) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[SlopesCompanion](#ref-SlopesCompanion)]. An *averaging profile* there is a real compactly supported smooth function $\varphi$ of mass one with moments of orders $1,2,3$ equal to zero, and, for some $G\ge1$, $$\lVert\varphi^{(r)}\rVert_\infty
 \le \bigl(G(r+2)\bigr)^{G(r+2)}\qquad(r\ge0).$$ The constant $G$ may depend on the profile. We need the following two precise conclusions.

<a id="stmt-prop-smooth-input"></a>
<div id="prop:smooth-input" class="proposition">

**Proposition 3** (Smooth triple averages, companion input). *Let $R$ be any invertible probability-preserving transformation, and let $b_1,b_2,b_3$ be distinct nonzero integers with greatest common divisor one. For bounded $g_1,g_2,g_3$ and every averaging profile, $$V_k^\varphi(y)=\sum_{l\in\mathbb Z}2^{-k}\varphi(l/2^k)
              \prod_{j=1}^3g_j(R^{b_jl}y)$$ converges almost everywhere as $k\to\infty$. Moreover, for each $c\in[1,2]$ and $\epsilon>0$ there is such a profile with $$\lVert\varphi-c^{-1}\mathbf 1_{(0,c]}\rVert_{L^1(\mathbb R)}<\epsilon.$$*

</div>

The approximation is (OpenAI 2026c, Lemma 3.9) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[SlopesCompanion](#ref-SlopesCompanion)]. Neither part requires mixing or ergodicity. We will not assume pointwise convergence of longer averages on arbitrary systems.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Relative weak mixing and distal factors

<a id="eq:rwm"></a>
We recall the structure facts in the form needed for the auxiliary systems. An extension of an invertible system $R$ over a factor $B$ is *relatively weakly mixing* if, for bounded $f,g$ with $\mathbb E[f\mid B]=0$, $$\label{eq:rwm}
 \frac1H\sum_{h=1}^H
 \lVert\mathbb E[\overline f(g\circ R^h)\mid B]\rVert_2^2\longrightarrow0.$$ An extension is *relatively compact* if the invariant finitely generated $L^\infty(B)$-modules span a dense subspace of its $L^2$ space. A module consists of finite sums $\sum_j a_jF_j$, with $a_j\in L^\infty(B)$ and fixed $F_j\in L^2$; invariance is under composition with both $R$ and $R^{-1}$. Individual modules need not be closed; see the corrected module characterization in (Jamneshan 2026, Theorem 2.5(ii)$'$) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[JamneshanCorrigendum2026](#ref-JamneshanCorrigendum2026)]. A *relatively distal* extension is a tower of relatively compact extensions and inverse limits. A system is *distal* if it is distal over the trivial factor. A joining is an invariant probability law on a product with the prescribed marginals.

<a id="stmt-prop-structure"></a>
<div id="prop:structure" class="proposition">

**Proposition 4** (Relative structure). *The following statements hold for standard probability-preserving systems, without an ergodicity assumption.*

1.  *Every system $Y$ has a maximal measure-distal factor $W_Y$, and $Y\to W_Y$ is relatively weakly mixing. Every distal factor pulls into the maximal distal factor of an extension. Automorphisms commuting with the transformation preserve $W_Y$.*

2.  *Factors and joinings of distal systems are distal. A distal system is relatively distal over each of its factors. Distality is equivalent for a transformation and any of its nonzero integer powers.*

3.  *Relative weak mixing passes to nonzero integer powers. The conditional product of relatively weakly mixing fibre laws over an invariant joining of their base laws is relatively weakly mixing over that joining.*

4.  *A relatively weakly mixing extension and a relatively distal extension of the same base are relatively disjoint: every invariant joining identifying their bases is the conditional product over that base.*

5.  *For a distal transformation $R$, every $d\ge1$, and bounded $g_1,\ldots,g_d$, the averages $$\frac1N\sum_{l=1}^N\prod_{i=1}^d g_i\circ R^{il}$$ converge almost everywhere.*

*For a flow, the maximal distal factor of time one is preserved by the flow, and its action at every nonzero rational time is distal.*

</div>

These are the conclusions of (OpenAI 2026a, Proposition 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[FourfoldCompanion](#ref-FourfoldCompanion)]. They include the nonergodic form of the Furstenberg–Zimmer structure theorem (Zimmer 1976; Jamneshan 2023) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Zimmer1976](#ref-Zimmer1976), [Jamneshan2023](#ref-Jamneshan2023)] and the pointwise theorem for distal systems (Huang, Shao, and Ye 2019, Theorem C) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[HuangShaoYe2019](#ref-HuangShaoYe2019)]. The cited proposition gives the component-disintegration argument needed for the latter in the nonergodic setting. In the flow assertion, invariance of $W_Y$ follows because each time commutes with time one. If $r=p/q\ne0$ is rational, the $q$th power of its action is the distal action at integer time $p$, so the power assertion applies.

The following short argument, from (OpenAI 2026a, Lemma 2.3) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[FourfoldCompanion](#ref-FourfoldCompanion)], will also fix the norm-convergence input explicitly.

<a id="stmt-lem-relative-norm"></a>
<div id="lem:relative-norm" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Relative norm convergence). *Suppose $Y\to B$ is relatively weakly mixing for $R$. For distinct nonzero integers $a_1,\ldots,a_d$ and bounded $G_1,\ldots,G_d$, <a id="eq:relative-norm"></a>$$\label{eq:relative-norm}
 \left\|\frac1N\sum_{l=1}^N
 \left(\prod_{i=1}^dG_i\circ R^{a_il}
       -\prod_{i=1}^d\mathbb E[G_i\mid B]\circ R^{a_il}\right)\right\|_2
 \longrightarrow0.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-relative-norm"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-relative-norm</summary>

*Proof.* By telescoping and scaling, it suffices to show that the average of $v_l=\prod_i G_i\circ R^{a_il}$ tends to zero when all functions are bounded by one and one of them, $G_s$, is centered over $B$. Induct on $d$. For fixed $h\ge1$ put $F_{i,h}=\overline{G_i}(G_i\circ R^{a_ih})$. Invariance gives $$\langle v_l,v_{l+h}\rangle
 =\int_Y F_{s,h}\prod_{i\ne s}
                  F_{i,h}\circ R^{(a_i-a_s)l}\,dm_Y.$$ The induction hypothesis replaces the factors indexed by $i\ne s$ in their average by their conditional expectations over $B$. The remaining product is $B$-measurable and bounded by one; hence $$\limsup_{N\to\infty}
 \left|\frac1N\sum_{l=1}^N\langle v_l,v_{l+h}\rangle\right|
 \le\lVert\mathbb E[F_{s,h}\mid B]\rVert_1.$$ For $d=1$ this bound holds directly. The Hilbert-space van der Corput inequality now bounds the limiting squared norm by a constant times $$\frac1H+\frac1H\sum_{h=1}^H\lVert\mathbb E[F_{s,h}\mid B]\rVert_1.$$ Relative weak mixing for $R^{a_s}$ and Cauchy–Schwarz make this expression tend to zero as $H\to\infty$. ◻

</details>

For positive slopes, inserting functions $1$ in omitted positions and using Proposition <a href="#prop:structure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:structure">4</a>(5) shows that the distal-factor averages have an $L^2$ limit. Thus Lemma <a href="#lem:relative-norm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:relative-norm">5</a> gives an $L^2$ limit for arbitrary bounded tuples with distinct positive slopes on every standard system. On the original mixing system, the trivial factor already satisfies <a href="#eq:rwm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rwm">[eq:rwm]</a>; the limit is the product of the means.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Triple convergence along all lengths

<a id="stmt-lem-triple-convergence"></a>
<div id="lem:triple-convergence" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6** (Triple pointwise convergence). *On any standard invertible probability-preserving system, averages of at most three bounded functions at distinct positive integer slopes converge almost everywhere along all positive integer lengths.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-triple-convergence"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-triple-convergence</summary>

*Proof.* Add constant-one functions if necessary to have three distinct positive slopes, write them as $gb_1,gb_2,gb_3$ with $\gcd(b_1,b_2,b_3)=1$, and apply Proposition <a href="#prop:smooth-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:smooth-input">3</a> to $S=R^g$. By scaling, suppose the three functions are bounded by one, and put $F_l=\prod_{j=1}^3g_j\circ S^{b_jl}$.

For each rational $c\in[1,2]$, choose a sequence of profiles tending in $L^1$ to $h_c=c^{-1}\mathbf1_{(0,c]}$. On a common conull set all the corresponding smooth dyadic averages converge. For $N_k=\lfloor c2^k\rfloor$, the difference between the ordinary average at $N_k$ and $V_k^\varphi$ is at most $$\frac1{c2^k}
 +2^{-k}\sum_{l\in\mathbb Z}|h_c(l/2^k)-\varphi(l/2^k)|.$$ The last sum tends to $\lVert h_c-\varphi\rVert_1$ by Riemann sums. Arbitrarily close profiles therefore give the Cauchy property on each of these scales. The $L^2$ limit established above identifies all their almost-everywhere limits with the same function.

<a id="eq:length-change"></a>
For any bounded sequence $|F_l|\le1$, changing the averaging length from $N$ to $M$ changes the average by at most $$\label{eq:length-change}
                 \frac{2|M-N|}{\max(M,N)}.$$ For $2^k\le N<2^{k+1}$, compare $N$ with $\lfloor c2^k\rfloor$ on a finite rational grid in $[1,2]$. Equation <a href="#eq:length-change" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:length-change">[eq:length-change]</a> makes the limiting error arbitrarily small as the grid mesh tends to zero. This proves convergence along all integer lengths. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:selected"></a>
### Selected lengths and their joining laws

We assume that the asserted pointwise convergence fails for the fixed set of slopes $I=\{1,\ldots,n\}$. This section records that failure in a probability law and constructs compatible laws on all flow extensions. The construction retains a positive correlation while forcing every one-, two-, or three-slot marginal to have its ordinary conditional law. We follow the selector and joining construction in (OpenAI 2026a, secs. 3–6) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[FourfoldCompanion](#ref-FourfoldCompanion)], keeping the extension and conditioning properties explicit for the arbitrary number of slots.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### A positive witness at selected lengths

<a id="eq:failure-witness"></a>
Expanding each function into its mean and centered part, and then into real and imaginary parts, gives a nonempty $J_0\subseteq I$ and real functions $f_i$, $i\in J_0$, such that $$\int_X f_i\,\,d\mu=0,\qquad \lVert f_i\rVert_\infty\le1,
  \qquad
  B_L(x)=\frac1L\sum_{l=1}^L\prod_{i\in J_0}f_i(T^{il}x)$$ does not tend to zero on a set of positive measure. Here and below $B_L$ has an integer subscript. Lemma <a href="#lem:relative-norm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:relative-norm">5</a>, applied over the trivial factor, gives $B_L\to0$ in $L^2(\mu)$. After changing the sign of one $f_i$, there are $\delta>0$ and a measurable set $E_*\subseteq X$ of positive measure such that $$\label{eq:failure-witness}
  B_L(x)>2\delta\quad\text{for arbitrarily large integers }L,
  \qquad x\in E_*.$$

We use the roof-one suspension <a id="eq:suspension"></a>$$\label{eq:suspension}
  C=X\times[0,1),\qquad m_C=\mu\otimes\,du,
  \qquad
  S_C^t(x,u)=\bigl(T^{\lfloor u+t\rfloor}x,\{u+t\}\bigr).$$ Write $\xi(x,u)=x$ and $\upsilon(x,u)=u$, regarding $\upsilon$ also as a coordinate in the circle $\mathbb R/\mathbb Z$.

<a id="stmt-lem-selected-witness"></a>
<div id="lem:selected-witness" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:selected-positive-limit"></a>
**Lemma 7** (Selected positive witness). *There are a finite set $F\subseteq[1,2]$ containing $1$ and measurable functions $N_a:C\to[2^a,\infty)$, $a\in\mathbb N$, independent of the phase, with <a id="eq:selector-grid"></a>$$\label{eq:selector-grid}
  N_a(x,u)\in\{c2^k:c\in F,\ k\ge a\},\qquad
  N_a(x,u)=2^a\quad(x\notin E_*),$$ such that $B_{\lfloor N_a(x,u)\rfloor}(x)>\delta$ on $E_*$. Moreover, there is a continuous nonnegative function $\Psi$ on $(\mathbb R/\mathbb Z)^I$ for which $$\label{eq:selected-positive-limit}
 \liminf_{a\to\infty}\int_C\frac1{N_a(z)}
 \int_0^{N_a(z)}
 \Psi\bigl((\upsilon(S_C^{it}z))_{i\in I}\bigr)
 \prod_{i\in J_0}f_i\bigl(\xi(S_C^{it}z)\bigr)
 \,\,dt\,\,dm_C(z)>0.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-selected-witness"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-selected-witness</summary>

*Proof.* By <a href="#eq:length-change" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:length-change">[eq:length-change]</a>, changing the length from $L$ to $M$ changes an average of a sequence bounded by one by at most $2\lvert M-L\rvert/\max(M,L)$. Choose $F$ to be a sufficiently fine finite grid in $[1,2]$. For every sufficiently large integer $L$, some $c\in F$ and $k=\lfloor\log_2L\rfloor$ satisfy $$\lvert B_L(x)-B_{\lfloor c2^k\rfloor}(x)\rvert<\delta
  \qquad\text{for every }x.$$ Equation <a href="#eq:failure-witness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:failure-witness">[eq:failure-witness]</a> therefore supplies, for each $x\in E_*$ and $a$, a pair $(c,k)$ with $k\ge a$ at which the latter average exceeds $\delta$. Choose the first such pair in a fixed enumeration. This is a measurable choice from a countable set and defines $N_a$ on $E_*$; use $2^a$ elsewhere.

Choose continuous nonnegative circle functions $\alpha,\beta$, neither identically zero, supported respectively inside $(1/4,1/2)$ and $(0,1/(4n))$. Set <a id="eq:phase-witness"></a>$$\label{eq:phase-witness}
 \Psi(v)=\alpha(2v_1-v_2)\beta(v_2-v_1),
 \qquad v=(v_i)_{i\in I}\in(\mathbb R/\mathbb Z)^I.$$ For $z=(x,u)$ and $t=l+s$, where $l\in\mathbb Z$ and $0\le s<1$, the phase factor is $\alpha(u)\beta(s)$. If it is nonzero, then $0<u+is<1$ for every $i\in I$, so $\xi(S_C^{it}z)=T^{il}x$. Summing complete unit intervals gives, uniformly in $z$ and real $L\ge2$, <a id="eq:suspension-witness"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 &\frac1L\int_0^L
 \Psi\bigl((\upsilon(S_C^{it}z))_i\bigr)
 \prod_{i\in J_0}f_i\bigl(\xi(S_C^{it}z)\bigr)\,\,dt
 \notag\\
 &\hspace{18mm}
 =\alpha(u)\left(\int_0^1\beta(s)\,\,ds\right)
 B_{\lfloor L\rfloor}(x)+O(L^{-1}).
 \label{eq:suspension-witness}
\end{aligned}$$ The error includes the incomplete last interval, the change from indices $0,\ldots,\lfloor L\rfloor-1$ to $1,\ldots,\lfloor L\rfloor$, and the normalization. On $E_*$ the main term at $L=N_a(z)$ has integral at least $$\delta\mu(E_*)
 \left(\int_0^1\alpha(u)\,\,du\right)
 \left(\int_0^1\beta(s)\,\,ds\right)>0.$$ On $X\setminus E_*$ its integral tends to zero because $N_a=2^a$ there and $B_{2^a}\to0$ in $L^2(\mu)$. The uniform error is $O(2^{-a})$, proving <a href="#eq:selected-positive-limit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:selected-positive-limit">[eq:selected-positive-limit]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Selected laws on all extensions

Fix these selectors and a free ultrafilter $\mathcal U$ on $\mathbb N$. A *flow over $C$* is a standard probability space $(Y,m_Y)$ with a jointly Borel probability-preserving action $S_Y^t$, $t\in\mathbb R$, and a probability-preserving factor map $\pi_Y:Y\to C$ satisfying $\pi_Y S_Y^t=S_C^t\pi_Y$ modulo null sets for each fixed $t$. An extension $\rho:Y'\to Y$ of such flows is a probability-preserving factor map, equivariant in the same sense, with $\pi_{Y'}=\pi_Y\rho$ modulo null sets. We do not require a common conull set on which every real-time factor identity holds. Joint measurability and Fubini justify their uses inside time integrals.

<a id="stmt-prop-selected-law"></a>
<div id="prop:selected-law" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:selected-law"></a>
**Proposition 8** (Selected joining laws). *Every flow $Y$ over $C$ has a unique probability law $\lambda_Y$ on $Y^I$ satisfying, for all bounded measurable functions $G_i$ on $Y$, $$\label{eq:selected-law}
 \int_{Y^I}\prod_{i\in I}G_i(y_i)\,\,d\lambda_Y
 =\lim_{a\to\mathcal U}\int_Y\frac1{N_a(\pi_Yy)}
   \int_0^{N_a(\pi_Yy)}\prod_{i\in I}G_i(S_Y^{it}y)
   \,\,dt\,\,dm_Y(y).$$ Its marginals are $m_Y$, and it is invariant under $(y_i)_i\mapsto(S_Y^{it}y_i)_i$, $t\in\mathbb R$. If $\rho:Y'\to Y$ is an extension over $C$, then $(\rho^I)_*\lambda_{Y'}=\lambda_Y$.*

*Countable inverse limits of extensions over $C$ exist in this category. The selected law at such a limit projects to the selected law at every stage. Pullbacks of the stage selected-law spaces have dense union in $L^2$ of the limit selected law.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-selected-law"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-selected-law</summary>

*Proof.* For each $a$ the expression before the ultralimit defines a genuine probability measure $\lambda_{Y,a}$ on $Y^I$, by integrating the measurable orbit map over the indicated finite time interval. We first show that each of its marginals converges against every bounded measurable test $G$ to $m_Y$. The one-slot continuous-time averages $$\frac1L\int_0^L G(S_Y^{it}y)\,\,dt$$ converge almost surely as $L\to\infty$. For example, apply Birkhoff’s theorem for $S_Y^i$ to the function $y\mapsto\int_0^1G(S_Y^{is}y)\,\,ds$ and discard the incomplete unit interval. Their almost-sure limit has integral $\int G\,\,dm_Y$, since every deterministic average has that integral. As $N_a(\pi_Yy)\ge2^a$, bounded convergence gives the same integral limit at the selected lengths.

Embed $Y$ as a Borel subset of a compact metrizable space $K$. Ultralimits of the $\lambda_{Y,a}$ against continuous functions on $K^I$ give a positive normalized functional, hence a probability measure on $K^I$. Its marginals are $m_Y$, by the preceding paragraph, so it is concentrated on $Y^I$. Call the resulting law $\lambda_Y$. To obtain <a href="#eq:selected-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:selected-law">[eq:selected-law]</a> for arbitrary bounded slot functions, normalize their bounds to one and approximate each in $L^1(m_Y)$ by continuous functions on $K$ with the same bound. The difference between the two product integrals is bounded by the sum of the single-slot errors. Under $\lambda_Y$ these errors are the $L^1(m_Y)$ errors, while under $\lambda_{Y,a}$ they converge to those errors by the marginal convergence already proved. Letting the errors tend to zero proves the formula. Product tests determine a probability measure on $Y^I$, giving uniqueness. In particular, all these identities depend only on the almost-everywhere classes of their slot functions.

Applying the product action at a fixed time $t_0$ shifts the time interval in <a href="#eq:selected-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:selected-law">[eq:selected-law]</a> by $t_0$ without changing its length or its initial-point selector. For slot functions bounded by one the error is at most $2\lvert t_0\rvert 2^{-a}$, proving invariance. For an extension $\rho$, substitute $G_i\circ\rho$ in the formula. Equivariance, the equality of the selectors, and Fubini identify its right-hand side with that for $Y$, proving functoriality.

For a tower $Y_0\leftarrow Y_1\leftarrow\cdots$, take the full countable product of the underlying standard spaces, with the consistent inverse-limit probability measure and coordinatewise flow. This is a jointly Borel action. A cylinder through stage $r$ has probability computed from $m_{Y_r}$ by the finitely many bonding maps. At any fixed time their equivariance and preservation of $m_{Y_r}$ leave that probability unchanged. Cylinders determine the measure, so every fixed time preserves it. The measure is concentrated on compatible sequences; using the full product avoids any need for one compatibility set invariant at all real times. The stage projections are factor maps modulo null sets, and projection through $Y_0$ gives the factor onto $C$. Functoriality now gives the asserted selected-law projections. Finally, the stage coordinates generate the inverse-limit sigma-algebra. The compatibility relations hold under the selected law slot by slot because its marginals are the inverse-limit probability measure. Thus the pulled-back stage sigma-algebras on the selected-law space are increasing and generate it modulo null sets. The usual $L^2$ approximation by an increasing sequence of sigma-algebras proves density. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Distal parameters and small marginals

For a flow $Y$ over $C$, let $\omega_Y:Y\to W_Y$ be its maximal measure-distal factor for time one, and let $\nu_Y=(\omega_Y)_*m_Y$. Write $m_{Y,w}$ for its conditional probability measures, so that $$m_Y=\int_{W_Y}m_{Y,w}\,\,d\nu_Y(w).$$ Under $\lambda_Y$ put <a id="eq:distal-parameters"></a>$$\label{eq:distal-parameters}
 P_i=\omega_Y(y_i),\qquad P=(P_i)_{i\in I}.$$ Proposition <a href="#prop:structure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:structure">4</a> ensures that $W_Y$ is preserved by every flow time. Such statements about factor sigma-algebras are understood modulo null sets; all time integrals below are evaluated on the original jointly Borel flow. If $\rho:Y'\to Y$ is an extension, the pullback of $W_Y$ is distal for time one and hence is contained in $W_{Y'}$. Consequently the old parameter array is a measurable function of the new parameter array on selected-law spaces.

<a id="stmt-prop-selected-parameters"></a>
<div id="prop:selected-parameters" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:deterministic-parameters"></a>
<a id="eq:small-conditional-law"></a>
**Proposition 9** (Parameter and small-marginal laws). *For every flow $Y$ over $C$ and bounded measurable $H_i$ on $W_Y$, $$\label{eq:deterministic-parameters}
 \mathbb E_{\lambda_Y}\prod_{i\in I}H_i(P_i)
 =\lim_{L\to\infty}\int_Y\frac1L\int_0^L
   \prod_{i\in I}H_i\bigl(\omega_Y(S_Y^{it}y)\bigr)
   \,\,dt\,\,dm_Y(y).$$ For every nonempty $J\subseteq I$ with $\lvert J\rvert\le3$, $$\label{eq:small-conditional-law}
 \mathcal L_{\lambda_Y}\bigl((y_i)_{i\in J}\mid P\bigr)
   =\bigotimes_{i\in J}m_{Y,P_i}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-selected-parameters"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-selected-parameters</summary>

*Proof.* We first explain the passage from discrete to continuous time used in both assertions. Write $t=l+s$, with $l\ge0$ an integer and $0\le s<1$. For each fixed $s$, a continuous-time product becomes the discrete product at slopes $i$ for the shifted slot functions $G_i\circ S_Y^{is}$. Whenever the corresponding discrete averages converge almost surely, Fubini and bounded convergence show that their integrals in $s$ converge almost surely. Starting the discrete average at $l=0$ rather than $l=1$, and dropping an incomplete final unit interval, each has an error tending to zero. The resulting continuous-time convergence holds along all real lengths $L$.

Apply this observation to functions from $W_Y$. Their shifts still belong to $W_Y$, and pointwise convergence on the distal factor is part of Proposition <a href="#prop:structure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:structure">4</a>. Thus the time averages in <a href="#eq:deterministic-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:deterministic-parameters">[eq:deterministic-parameters]</a> converge almost surely along all lengths, and therefore at the selected lengths as well. Bounded convergence and <a href="#eq:selected-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:selected-law">[eq:selected-law]</a> prove that equation.

<a id="eq:small-own-parameters"></a>
For $\lvert J\rvert\le3$, Lemma <a href="#lem:triple-convergence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:triple-convergence">6</a> and the same continuous-time argument show that the selected $J$-marginal equals its deterministic-length integrated law. The extension $Y\to W_Y$ is relatively weakly mixing. Apply Lemma <a href="#lem:relative-norm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:relative-norm">5</a> to the distinct integer slopes $i\in J$ and the shifted functions $G_i\circ S_Y^{is}$, for each fixed $s$. Conditional expectation commutes with these shifts because $W_Y$ is preserved. Integration in $s$, using the uniform norm bound and bounded convergence, gives <a id="eq:small-replacement"></a>$$\label{eq:small-replacement}
 \mathbb E_{\lambda_Y}\prod_{i\in J}G_i(y_i)
 =\mathbb E_{\lambda_Y}\prod_{i\in J}
    \mathbb E_{m_Y}[G_i\mid W_Y](y_i).$$ Indeed the difference of the deterministic averages tends to zero in $L^2(m_Y)$, so also in their integrated values. Applying this identity to $G_i$ multiplied by arbitrary bounded functions of $P_i$ yields $$\label{eq:small-own-parameters}
 \mathcal L_{\lambda_Y}\bigl((y_i)_{i\in J}\mid(P_i)_{i\in J}\bigr)
 =\bigotimes_{i\in J}m_{Y,P_i}.$$

Conditioning on the full parameter array requires one more step; it does not follow from <a href="#eq:small-own-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:small-own-parameters">[eq:small-own-parameters]</a> alone. Use the invariant transformation with speed $i$ in slot $i$. By the stability under nonzero powers and conditional products in Proposition <a href="#prop:structure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:structure">4</a>, the $J$-marginal system in <a href="#eq:small-own-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:small-own-parameters">[eq:small-own-parameters]</a> is relatively weakly mixing over its parameter law. The full $P$-system is a joining of the distal systems $W_Y$ at these integer powers. It is distal, and is therefore relatively distal over the subarray $(P_i)_{i\in J}$. Their joint law under $\lambda_Y$ is an invariant joining identifying that common subarray. Relative disjointness in Proposition <a href="#prop:structure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:structure">4</a> makes this joining conditionally independent over the subarray, which is precisely <a href="#eq:small-conditional-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:small-conditional-law">[eq:small-conditional-law]</a>. ◻

</details>

The full parameter law is thus unaffected by the selectors, although the full state law need not be. We now turn that state law into a new flow while preserving one chosen slot as the original system.

<a id="stmt-lem-normalized-joining"></a>
<div id="lem:normalized-joining" class="lemma">

**Lemma 10** (Normalized joining flow). *For $b\in I$, define <a id="eq:normalized-joining"></a>$$\label{eq:normalized-joining}
 \mathsf M_b(Y)=
 \left(Y^I,\lambda_Y,
   S_{\mathsf M_b(Y)}^t(y_i)_i=(S_Y^{it/b}y_i)_i\right).$$ This is a standard probability-preserving flow over $C$ via $(y_i)_i\mapsto\pi_Y(y_b)$, and projection onto slot $b$ is an extension onto $Y$. The array $P$ belongs to the maximal measure-distal factor of $\mathsf M_b(Y)$ for time one.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-normalized-joining"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-normalized-joining</summary>

*Proof.* Invariance follows from Proposition <a href="#prop:selected-law" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:selected-law">8</a>, after rescaling the time parameter by $1/b$. Slot $b$ has speed one and marginal $m_Y$, giving the stated factor maps. In slot $i$ the $W_Y$-factor is distal for time $i/b$, by Proposition <a href="#prop:structure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:structure">4</a>. The law of $P$ is an invariant joining of these rational-time distal systems, hence is distal. Maximality of the distal factor gives the last assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-positive-witness"></a>
<div id="lem:positive-witness" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:positive-witness"></a>
**Lemma 11** (The retained positive correlation). *With the functions and phase test of Lemma <a href="#lem:selected-witness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:selected-witness">7</a>, $$\label{eq:positive-witness}
 \int_{C^I}\Psi\bigl((\upsilon(z_i))_i\bigr)
    \prod_{i\in J_0}f_i\bigl(\xi(z_i)\bigr)\,\,d\lambda_C>0.$$ The phase array in this integral is measurable in the distal parameter array of $C^I$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-positive-witness"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-positive-witness</summary>

*Proof.* Continuous functions on the compact product of circles are uniformly approximable by finite sums of products of continuous one-coordinate functions. Apply this to $\Psi$, use <a href="#eq:selected-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:selected-law">[eq:selected-law]</a> for each resulting product, and then use the strictly positive lower limit in <a href="#eq:selected-positive-limit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:selected-positive-limit">[eq:selected-positive-limit]</a>. Finally, time one fixes the phase on $C$, so the phase factor is distal and lies in $W_C$. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:channels"></a>
### Channels and closure under selected laws

The selected length is determined by the original point in the suspension. It can therefore change the joint law of other coordinates, even when their unconditioned law is a product. We organize those coordinates in three groups. Independence of the original point from any two groups will control the effect of selecting a length on all three groups. This channel construction and its closure argument adapt (OpenAI 2026a, sec. 5) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[FourfoldCompanion](#ref-FourfoldCompanion)]; the proof below permits every fixed finite set of slots $I=\{1,\ldots,n\}$.

<a id="stmt-def-channel"></a>
<div id="def:channel" class="definition">

<a id="eq:channel-laws"></a>
**Definition 12** (Channel). A *channel* is a flow $Y$ over the suspension $C$, with factor map $\pi_Y:Y\to C$, together with a finite list of probability-preserving maps $D_\gamma:Y\to C$, called *outputs*, and positive rational numbers $q_\gamma$, such that $$D_\gamma(S_Y^t y)=S_C^{q_\gamma t}D_\gamma(y)$$ for each fixed $t$, modulo null sets. Partition the output indices into three groups, numbered $1,2,3$; empty groups are allowed. Set $$x_0=\xi\circ\pi_Y,\qquad x_\gamma=\xi\circ D_\gamma,
 \qquad d_Y=(x_\gamma)_\gamma,$$ and let $d_{Y,H}$ be the sublist from groups in $H\subseteq\{1,2,3\}$. Let $V$ collect the phase coordinates of $\pi_Y$ and all the $D_\gamma$. Under the measure $m_Y$ require $$\label{eq:channel-laws}
 \begin{split}
  \mathcal L(d_Y\mid V)&=\bigotimes_\gamma\mu,\\
  \mathcal L(x_0,d_{Y,H}\mid V)&=
       \mu\otimes\bigotimes_{\gamma\text{ in groups }H}\mu,
       \qquad H\subsetneq\{1,2,3\}.
 \end{split}$$ An empty spatial list has the one-point law.

</div>

Thus the spatial outputs have a product law independent of the phases. The original spatial coordinate has a product law with the outputs in any proper collection of groups, but no independence from the complete output list is required. The suspension $C$, with no outputs, is a channel.

<a id="stmt-lem-channel-conditioning"></a>
<div id="lem:channel-conditioning" class="lemma">

**Lemma 13** (Conditioning and extensions). *For a channel $Y$, both identities in <a href="#eq:channel-laws" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:channel-laws">[eq:channel-laws]</a> hold with conditioning on its maximal measure-distal factor $W_Y$ in place of $V$. Every flow extension of $Y$ over $C$ is a channel with the pulled back output data, and the same strengthening holds there.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-channel-conditioning"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-channel-conditioning</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a positive integer $h$ such that $hq_\gamma$ is an integer for each output. The phase vector $V$ is a factor fixed by time $h$, and its time-one action is distal, so $V$ belongs to $W_Y$. For any spatial tuple appearing in <a href="#eq:channel-laws" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:channel-laws">[eq:channel-laws]</a>, its joint factor with $V$ has product law over $V$. At time $h$ the spatial coordinates evolve by a product of positive integer powers of $T$. This product is mixing: the assertion follows first for product tests from mixing of each power, and then for all bounded tests by approximation. Consequently this factor is relatively weakly mixing over $V$ for time $h$.

On the other hand, $W_Y$ is distal for time $h$ and relatively distal over $V$. Relative disjointness, in the form of (OpenAI 2026a, Proposition 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[FourfoldCompanion](#ref-FourfoldCompanion)], makes the spatial factor and $W_Y$ relatively independent over $V$. Its conditional spatial law given $W_Y$ is therefore the product law in <a href="#eq:channel-laws" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:channel-laws">[eq:channel-laws]</a>. Pullback to an extension preserves the joint laws of the original data, proving the channel identities there; the same argument gives their stronger conditioning. ◻

</details>

We now prove the closure property that will permit repeated extensions. Recall from Lemma <a href="#lem:normalized-joining" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:normalized-joining">10</a> that $\mathsf M_b(Y)$ has state space $Y^I$, measure $\lambda_Y$, and flow $(y_i)_i\mapsto(S_Y^{it/b}y_i)_i$.

<a id="stmt-prop-channel-closure"></a>
<div id="prop:channel-closure" class="proposition">

**Proposition 14** (Channel closure). *Let $Y$ be a channel and $b\in I$. On $\mathsf M_b(Y)$ take $\pi_Y(y_b)$ as original factor and take every $D_\gamma(y_i)$, $i\in I$, as an output, in the old group of $\gamma$. These data make $\mathsf M_b(Y)$ a channel.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-channel-closure"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-channel-closure</summary>

*Proof.* Each new output is probability-preserving and has positive rational speed $iq_\gamma/b$. It remains to prove the two conditional laws. Test them with products of bounded spatial functions and products of bounded phase functions. Such tests determine the laws. Expanding each spatial function into its mean and centered part, and scaling, reduces the proof to real spatial functions that are either $1$ or centered and bounded in absolute value by $1$. We may also bound every phase function by $1$. Whenever at least one spatial factor is centered, the tested integral must be zero. A test using the new original coordinate uses outputs from only a proper set of groups.

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
###### Time intervals and offsets.

<a id="eq:channel-times"></a>
Use the selected-law formula <a href="#eq:selected-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:selected-law">[eq:selected-law]</a> and condition on the old phase vector $V$ under $m_Y$. Fix an integer $h>0$ clearing all output-speed denominators and write $t=hl+s$, where $l\ge0$ is an integer and $0\le s<h$. For fixed $V,s$, all phase weights in the test are independent of $l$. The spatial function for output $\gamma$ at slot $i$ is evaluated at $$\label{eq:channel-times}
 T^{e_\gamma i l+a_{\gamma,i}}x_\gamma,
 \qquad e_\gamma=hq_\gamma\in\mathbb N.$$ The offsets $a_{\gamma,i}$ depend on $V,s$ and range over a fixed finite set, by the suspension formula. If the original-factor test is present, its argument is $T^{hbl+a_0}x_0$, with the same finite-offset property. Equivariance and Fubini justify these formulas in the time integrals.

On an event where the selected length is $N=c2^k$, $c\in F$ and $k\ge a$, put $m=\lfloor c2^k/h\rfloor$. Replacing the normalized time integral by the average over the $m$ complete intervals, followed by integration against $ds/h$, has error at most $2h2^{-a}$. For all sufficiently large $a$ we have $m\ge1$.

<a id="pm-h-15"></a>
###### Tests using a proper set of groups.

Given $V$, the relevant old output coordinates have product law and are independent of $x_0$, by <a href="#eq:channel-laws" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:channel-laws">[eq:channel-laws]</a>. The selector is determined by $V,x_0$. Suppose first that some output occurrence is centered. For fixed $V,s,x_0$, integrate the spatial output product at index $l$. The expectation factors over the old output indices $\gamma$. For an index having a centered occurrence, the times <a href="#eq:channel-times" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:channel-times">[eq:channel-times]</a> in distinct slots separate as $l\to\infty$.
